日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

日本：設備投資は高水準を維持。製造業は業績対比でなお上振れ余地
4
 2026年度の設備投資計画は前年度比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持
― 製造業は6月調査からほぼ横ばいの同＋7.1％。非製造業は同＋11.8％と1.1％Ptの上方修正
 AI需要による好業績を踏まえれば製造業の投資計画にはやや慎重さがにじむ内容
― 一方、生産・営業用設備判断DIは2025年度後半以降、設備不足方向への動きが鮮明。高水準の内部資金も踏まえれ
ば、増産投資などを中心に今後の設備投資が上振れる余地
― なお、今後の利上げが企業の投資姿勢に及ぼす影響には留意
25年度後半以降、急速
な設備不足超を示唆

日本：企業の価格設定姿勢はなお強い。仕入価格再上昇にも警戒
5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続
―6・7月の油価下落等を背景に素材業種の仕入価格判断DIが低下。対して販売価格判断DIは横ばい。コスト低下局面で
も販売価格を維持しており、企業利益の押し上げ要因に
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しも2％台半ばと、日銀の物価目標を上回る水準で高止まり
― 足元の原油価格再上昇やタンカー運賃高騰を受け、仕入価格が再び上昇する可能性には留意。企業の価格設定姿勢
が一段と強まるリスクも
販売価格判断DI・仕入価格判断DI（大企業・製造業） 物価全般の見通し（全規模・全産業）

日本：利上げでも企業金融は安定。企業部門の金利耐性を確認
6
 資金繰り判断は総じて底堅い。これまでの利上げの影響は限定的
― 利上げの影響が懸念された中小企業の資金繰り判断は横ばい圏の推移。価格転嫁の進展などを受けた事業環境の改
善が資金繰りを支えている構図
― 金利上昇局面でも銀行貸出は増加基調を維持しており、企業の資金需要も底堅く推移
 企業収益や資金繰りが金利上昇への耐性を示す中、金融環境はなお緩和的
― 市場のターミナルレート予想は2.5％程度まで上昇し、日銀の中立金利推計レンジ上限に接近

（%）
欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
EUの石油製品消費はディーゼル・軽油が中心

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

注目点②G7備蓄放出でもくすぶるディーゼル供給不安

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

9
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ

## Document 034: 2026年10月12日國際經濟金融週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月12日國際經濟金融週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (國際經濟金融週報)
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年十月十二日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

8

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

Greater China Technology Hardware

Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.

## Document 161: 5年消失25%岗位，照片冲洗员、文员等面临淘汰！高盛量化“新技术冲击就业”速度

Source PDF: all report pdf/5年消失25%岗位，照片冲洗员、文员等面临淘汰！高盛量化“新技术冲击就业”速度.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 取代⼈类的焦虑暂时退潮，但⾼盛警告：平静之下暗流涌动。若 AI 最终带来 15% ⽣产率
提升，过渡期内最多 9% 的员⼯将被替代；历史上受技术冲击最深的 25 类职业，五年内就业
平均萎缩 25% 。更危险的是，⼀旦经济⾛弱，企业将加速⽤ AI 压缩⼈⼒成本，冲击烈度可
能超出预期。
摘要：
今年春天， AI 取代⼈类⼯作的恐慌情绪⼀度达到顶峰。但随着美国经济展现出超预期的韧性，这
股焦虑已明显降温 ——AI 相关裁员公告数量从⾼点⼤幅回落，媒体关于 AI 导致失业的报道也退潮
⾄年初⽔平。然⽽，⾼盛提醒市场：冷静不等于安全， AI 对劳动⼒市场的冲击远未结束，只是在
等待⼀个更合适的时机爆发。
据追⻛交易台，⾼盛全球投资研究在 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇发布的报告中给出结论：如果 AI 最终带来 15% 的
⽣产率提升，历史经验显⽰，过渡期内最多约 9% 的员⼯可能暂时离开现有岗位；在技术让劳动
岗位变得冗余的职业中，就业⼈数五年平均下降约 25% 。
当前影响仍可⻅，但范围较窄。⾼盛称，AI 暴露度最⾼⾏业过去三个⽉就业增⻓较⻓期趋势少

约 1 万⼈，不过这⼀缺⼝⼤体被 AI 相关建筑岗位招聘增加所抵消。服务业横截⾯数据开始显⽰压
⼒， AI 采⽤率与失业率的相关性已转正且具统计显著性， AI 采⽤率与就业增⻓的相关性略为负
值，但统计显著性不⾜。
市场关注点也已转向投资回报。⾼盛称，当前资本开⽀预期意味着， AI ⽤⼾每年需在 AI 应⽤上⽀
出约 1 万亿美元，才能让超⼤规模云⼚商和应⽤供应商实现市场似乎已计⼊的利润。对投资者来
说，关键变量在于 AI 节省劳动⼒成本的速度，以及这种节省是否以裁员、离职和岗位消失体现。
1% ⽣产率冲击：失业率短期上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点
⾼盛⽤三种经验⽅法衡量技术对劳动⼒的历史冲击。第⼀种是总量层⾯的技术冲击模型。结果
显⽰，技术创新带来的劳动⽣产率每提升 1% ，失业率峰值会暂时上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点，约两
年后影响消退。
失业率变化会低估实际岗位冲击。部分被替代员⼯可能较快找到新⼯作，部分员⼯可能退出劳
动⼒市场，这会压低失业率指标对真实冲击的反映。⾼盛进⼀步拆分岗位流量后发现，每 1% 技

术驱动的⽣产率提升，会带来 0.5 ⾄ 0.6 个百分点的累计总岗位流失率上升，同时拖累总招聘率约
0.2 个百分点。
⾏业层⾯的结果相近。⾼盛使⽤⾏业层⾯经利⽤率调整的全要素⽣产率冲击数据后发现，每 1%
⽣产率提升，同样对应失业率上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点，总岗位流失率累计上升约 0.6 个百分点，
岗位获得率下降约 0.2 个百分点。
在⾼盛基准情形下， AI 最终可能带来 15% 的经济整体⽣产率提升。按上述历史关系估算， AI 过
渡期内最多约 9% 的员⼯可能从当前岗位被替代，招聘放缓还可能对整体就业形成约 3% 的额外拖
累。
25 个职业样本：技术冲击后五年就业平均减少 25%
⾼盛选取 25 个最容易受到 AI 技术冲击的职业，这些职业包括照⽚冲印员、校对员、档案⽂员
等，属于技术对劳动需求影响最⼤的样本。⾼盛称，这类样本更接近 AI 可能影响最深岗位的上限
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

情形。
结果显⽰，这 25 个职业在技术冲击后五年就业⼈数平均下降约 25% 。其中照⽚冲印员降幅最
⼤，五年岗位减少接近 60% ，数码摄影和相关技术直接压缩传统冲印需求。
⾼盛样本中的 25 个受技术冲击职业包括：
这些职业的共同点是，技术直接压缩了原有⼈⼯流程。照⽚冲印受到数码摄影冲击，校对受到
⽂字处理和拼写语法检查⼯具冲击，档案⽂员受到电⼦⽂档系统冲击，抄表员受到智能远程表
具冲击，旅⾏代理受到线上订票渠道冲击，银⾏柜员受到 ATM 和⼿机银⾏分流。
岗位减少主要来⾃分离率上升，招聘放缓贡献较⼩
⾼盛最重要的发现之⼀是，技术冲击下的就业收缩更多来⾃岗位分离。岗位分离包括离职、被
辞退等⼈员离开原岗位的情形，招聘减少也有影响，但幅度较⼩。
在 25 个职业样本中，有 13 个职业的年度总招聘率下降，平均降幅为 0.7 个百分点。相⽐之下，有
19 个职业的年度总分离率上升，平均升幅达到 3.8 个百分点。
联合考察招聘率和分离率后，⾼盛发现， 25 个职业中有 17 个职业显⽰岗位⼈数减少更偏向由分

离率上升驱动。这与总量模型和⾏业模型⼀致：技术冲击通常先体现在实际岗位流失，再体现
在招聘放缓。
这对市场跟踪 AI 冲击具有直接意义。⾼盛认为， AI 相关裁员应成为观察 AI 就业替代的⾼频指标。
若未来 AI 带来的⽣产率提升兑现，单纯观察招聘计划可能反应偏慢，裁员公告、岗位分离和职业

### Page 3

层⾯⼈数变化更能提供实时信号。
周期决定替代速度：经济放缓会放⼤ AI 冲击
⾼盛同时强调， AI 对就业的影响尚处早期。美国只有 23% 的机构已将 AI 纳⼊部分常规⼯作流程，
⽽且这⼀采⽤率仍会⾼估当前⾃动化程度，因为多数机构尚未把 AI 嵌⼊所有可应⽤流程。⾼盛
称，⼤部分 AI 驱动的⽣产率收益仍在未来。
历史数据显⽰，⾃动化冲击带有明显周期性。经济健康时，⾃动化相关劳动⼒扰动通常较有
限；经济转弱时，企业更倾向通过⾃动化压缩劳动⼒成本，岗位流失速度会加快。
⾏业模型显⽰，当整体失业率上升时，每 1% 技术驱动⽣产率提升对失业率的影响会⽐基准估算
⾼出 0.1 ⾄ 0.2 个百分点。职业层⾯样本也显⽰，在技术冲击后五年内，整体失业率每额外上升 1
个百分点，受冲击职业就业⼈数会额外下降约 4% 。
⾼盛的结论是，若经济活动保持健康， AI 劳动⼒转型⼤概率可控，冲击将逐步显现；若经济进⼊
周期性放缓， AI 相关⾃动化和岗位流失可能加速，并放⼤商业周期波动。对投资者⽽⾔，未来
需要同时跟踪 AI 资本开⽀、企业劳动成本节省、 AI 相关裁员公告和宏观就业周期。

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

## Document 266: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

Standard Chartered Bank

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 301: FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus FEMO's Steady Climb

Source PDF: all report pdf/FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus  FEMO's Steady Climb.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus  FEMO's Steady Climb
Date:    5 hours ago
FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus FEMO's Steady Climb

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus
FEMO's Steady Climb
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 19 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash
I. FOMO vs FEMO
Forecasting the stock market is easy! Just multiply the P/E (price-to-
earnings ratio) by E (earnings per share) to get P (the stock price index).
The hard part, of course, is getting the P/E and E right. We call the P/E
component FOMO (fear of missing out) and the E component FEMO
(fabulous earnings momentum). This year, investors have been on a
rollercoaster ride as FOMO has fluctuated mostly to the downside, while

FEMO has continued climbing.

### Page 3

More recently, concerns about AI investment returns and rising US
Treasury yields have tightened credit conditions, raising fears about the
sustainability of the AI investment boom and weighing on P/Es. Since
early October, multiple compression has outpaced continued earnings
gains, dragging many stocks lower.
Semiconductors have been at the center of the recent rout, with the
semiconductor stock price index plunging 15.7% over eight consecutive
sessions. However, FOMO staged a comeback today, with the Nasdaq

rising 1.1% and semiconductors gaining 1.6%, snapping their losing
streak. The FOMO rollercoaster continues, but FEMO remains firmly on
an upward trajectory.
Here is more:
(1) S&P 500. So far this year, forward earnings surged 31.0% ytd through
October 19, more than offsetting a 14.2% contraction in the forward P/E.
Consequently, the index is up 12.3% ytd (chart).
(2) Information Technology. The divergence is even more striking in the
S&P 500 Information Technology sector. Forward earnings has soared

65.5% ytd, while the forward P/E has contracted 25.6%, leaving the
sector's stock price index up 23.0% ytd (chart). Since early October,
however, falling P/Es have outpaced rising earnings, pulling IT stock
prices lower.

### Page 4

(3) Semiconductors. FEMO is incredibly strong in the S&P 500
Semiconductors industry, which now accounts for a record 55.0% of the IT
sector's forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
That implies a significant forward P/E discount to the rest of the IT sector
and to the S&P 500. The discount reflects concerns that rapidly rising
borrowing costs for highly leveraged AI infrastructure providers could slow
the AI investment boom and weaken demand for chips. Semiconductor

earnings continue to soar, but that's because capacity constraints are
boosting semiconductor prices, which should fall sooner or later as either
demand slows or capacity expands, or both.
(4) Healthy constraints. We view investors' growing concerns about the
AI boom as good news. Financial, social, political, and physical
constraints should reduce overinvestment risks and keep the boom on a
sustainable path. Recently, financial constraints have been in focus, with

### Page 5

CoreWeave's bond yields surging above 15% last week as the Bond
Vigilantes increasingly scrutinized AI borrowers. The bond market is
playing a welcome role in allocating capital more efficiently. Meanwhile,
the big hyperscalers remain the boom's main drivers, supported by strong
cash flows and ample access to capital. The AI rollercoaster may be
hitting speed bumps, but that will help keep it on a steadier path.
II. Industrial Production
The strength of the US economy continues to underpin strong earnings

growth. Industrial production rose 0.7% m/m in September, its biggest
gain since April, and 2.2% y/y, the strongest since September 2022
(charts). Leading the way higher was utilities output. That reflects rising
electricity demand from data centers and manufacturing, as well as
weather-related demand. Manufacturing output edged up 0.3%.

### Page 6

Since ChatGPT was released in late 2022, technology-related industrial
production has risen sharply to record highs (chart).
Defense and space equipment production also continues to increase at a
solid pace, supported by rising military spending and the rapid growth of
the space industry (chart).

### Page 7

III. Federal Deficit
Fiscal policy also continues to boost economic growth, though at the cost
of large federal deficits and mounting federal debt. The federal budget
deficit totaled $1.99 trillion over the 12 months through September, while
marketable Treasury debt increased by $2.12 trillion (chart).
Federal outlays reached $7.40 trillion, including $3.77 trillion for health
care, Medicare, and Social Security (chart). Notably, net interest payments

reached a record $1.05 trillion, exceeding defense spending of $970
billion.

### Page 8

Tax receipts totaled $5.40 trillion, supported by rising individual income
and payroll taxes as well as tariff revenues (chart). Corporate income tax
receipts have been depressed this year by the 100% depreciation
allowance granted by last year's One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBB).
IV. Foreign Capital Flows
Given Corporate America's FEMO, it is not surprising that foreign capital
continues to pour into US financial markets. Over the 12 months through

August, net foreign purchases of US equities totaled a whopping $1.01
trillion, followed by corporate bonds and Treasury notes and bonds (chart).
Apparently, foreign investors have a greater appetite for US equities and
corporate bonds than for US Treasuries.

### Page 9

Net foreign equity inflows surged to a record high, far exceeding previous
peaks (chart). Private investors accounted for $844 billion, while foreign
official institutions contributed another $165 billion, a remarkable
development given their historically limited participation in US equity
markets.
Foreigners purchased more US corporate bonds than US Treasury notes
and bonds over the past 12 months (chart).

### Page 10

clause.

## Document 310: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

### Page 8

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

## Document 311: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital

### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

### Page 3

reflected in other regions too. Today, a 2-standard deviation move in 10-year Treasury 
yields would equate to about 50bps over a month or 30bps over two weeks, similar to 
the adjustments in recent days which explains the pullback in equity prices.

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

In the US, in particular, the forward PE multiple for the S&P has come down from 22x at

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

Exhibit 6: US equities have underperformed other major regions since 2025

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

The combination of higher cost of capital and greater capital intensity in the tech sector 
has reduced the value of their future cash flows and triggered a de-rating in the biggest 
companies.  The dominant capex hyperscalers in the US now have a forward PE close to 
the average of the rest of the market.

8. Software companies have also de-rated on the back of fears of disruption from AI 
while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

Exhibit 10: Industrials now trade at higher valuations than Technology

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

### Page 9

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至 3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由 2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率 0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因 ，
貢獻整體 CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導 ，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性 ，需求面
通膨略有提高 ，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在 4%以上維持更久 。受
到油價高盤 、通膨僵固 ，與財政惡化 、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響 ，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中 ， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈 ，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大 、
歐元走弱 ，美元轉強 ，商品、黃金承壓 。法
國政治 不確定性讓 歐股修正 、法股最疲 ，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高 ，增加企業借貸成本

與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，Goldman Sachs，凱基證券整理，註：右上圖之大型雲端服務供應商(CSP)企業為Amazon、Alphabet、Microsoft、Oracle
► 美國第三季財報季展開 ，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現 。根據FactSet數據
顯示 ，標普500指數第三季獲利 成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐 ，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI 需求帶動 ， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股 ，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力 。高盛指出 ，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普 500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長 ，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響 ，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間 ，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面 。隨財報
數據陸續公布 ，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現 ，並維持正向財測 ，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價 ，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐
熱門議題
美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張

投資焦點
► 醫療保健 、醫療服務 與生物技術等行業 ，在
景氣不好時 ，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高 ，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天 ，股市波動風險上升 ，觀察
過去當 VIX恐慌指數上揚時 ，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯 ，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率 、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑 ，部分公司估值偏低 。當
臨床數據改善 、或導入AI篩選資料與 藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值 。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力 ，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線 ，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值 ，帶動整體產業估值回升 。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場 需求，
創新藥物 (如瘦瘦針 )讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場 2030年銷售額預估上調 15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。

醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健
市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響 ，由於商業雇主
贊助保險 (ESI)和平價醫療法案 (ACA)的自付
額增加 ，以及最高自付費用要求 ，較去年同
期提高 ，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數 、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善 。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健 ，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費 ，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限 )來擴大利潤空間 ，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合 ，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險 (ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去 20 年來的最高水準 。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加 ，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑 。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

近3個月漲幅和預估獲利呈正成長，醫療生技股佳
醫療保健和生技預估獲利明年明顯成長，有利股價續揚

預估今明年產業獲利差異，醫療保健和生技成長最大

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境使 短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金

融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 防禦搭配科技股，以槓鈴策略穩中求勝!

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Sources: ANZ, Westpac
Despite risk, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• Ongoing Middle East tensions are increasing volatility and raising energy, input
and shipping costs, while broadly supporting prices for agricultural products.

Impacts vary depending on idiosyncratic supply and demand conditions.
• Risks are exacerbated by the impact of a now confirmed super-sized El Niño,
which could slice off up to 1ppt of New Zealand’s GDP should on-farm
production of both milk and meat contract significantly.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg, with milk powder prices holding up better than we expected. El Niño
presents a clear upside risk to this, as it does not appear to have been reflected

in GDT auction prices to date.
• Meanwhile average New Zealand beef export prices should remain elevated over
the coming year, as lean trimming prices reverse recent declines. Sheepmeat
prices are set to ease on rising Australian supply, but remain at elevated levels.
• Kiwifruit and apple export prices are expected to remain elevated, supported
by strong demand for premium varieties, although increasing supply is likely to
keep prices largely flat rather than drive further gains.

• Export prices for New Zealand logs are set to remain broadly stable to slightly
firmer, supported by lower log supply and balanced Chinese inventories.

Southern Oscillation Index

Output in the agriculture, forestry and fishing

Source: Macrobond, Stats NZ, Westpac
%yr Index
La Niña
El Niño
Farming is well placed to navigate impacts of a strong El Niño.
• Scientists have now confirmed that the current El Niño event will be the

strongest on record. Temperatures in the Pacific Ocean are now 3.05C above the
average, exceeding the record set in 2015.
• A supersized El Niño increases the probability of drier weather in eastern regions
of New Zealand in coming months, with wetter conditions likelier in the west and
southern areas of the country.
• The potential impact on GDP could be significant. Our previous research has
shown a severe drought could slice off as much as 1ppt from economic growth.

• Much of that is will reflect lower agriculture production, particularly in dairy,
sheep and beef farms. Increased on-farm costs and processing disruptions are
also likely.
• A 1% loss in GDP is likely an upper end estimate of the El Niño impact. A key
consideration is soil moisture levels are currently high, implying some resilience
to future drier conditions.
• Farming practices have become more resilient to prolonged dry periods.
Irrigation is more widely used than in past dry periods and snowmelt levels

appear robust, which should be of particular benefit to Canterbury farms.

EL NIÑO

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Farm input costs are on the rise reflecting two key drivers: fuel and
fertiliser costs.
• Diesel and petrol prices have increased by about 50% compared to the same
period in 2025. While average prices fell in the September quarter, they closed

the quarter at an elevated level as tensions between the US and Iran increased
and broadened to encompass Saudi Arabia and the Yemeni Houthis.
• Fertiliser prices are also significantly higher. While the fertiliser market has
stabilised after a period of extreme volatility earlier this year, prices remain
highly sensitive to developments in the Middle East.
• Nitrogen (urea) prices have fallen substantially from a mid-year spike, with an
pick up in Chinese exports now boosting supply. These prices though remain

vulnerable to supply disruptions.
• By contrast, phosphate and other sulphur-based fertiliser prices are tracking
higher and remain at elevated levels, as supply constraints, trade restrictions
and disruptions to shipping offset the impact of softening demand in some
regions of the world.

Sources: Fonterra, Stats NZ, Westpac Westpac
forecasts
Farmgate milk price forecast revised up on strong protein demand.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg. This is above Fonterra’s forecast midpoint of $9.50/kg (range of $8.50-

10.50), but lower than futures market pricing of just over $10/kg.
• We have also opened our forecast for the 2027/28 season at $9.50/kg. Note that
there is a wide margin of uncertainty around the forecast at this stage, and we
will review as the next season approaches.
• Dairy prices at auction have generally held up better than our expectations,
although the performance has varied across products. Trends in global demand
are shifting away from fats and towards proteins, which has boosted demand

and prices for skim milk powder in particular.
• Our forecasts do not include an assumption about the impact of El Niño, which
presents a clear upside risk to prices and a downside risk to volumes. While the
futures market is pricing a strong lift in milk powder prices over the New Zealand
summer, there has been no apparent urgency to buy at the GDT auction.
Fonterra is forecasting a 2% lift in milk volumes over the rest of this season, on
top of what was already a strong increase last season.

• Global milk production also remains ample, although it has slowed in Europe
in recent months. El Niño is less likely to affect conditions among the major
producers in the Northern Hemisphere.

Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
Export beef prices to remain elevated over the next year.
• Beef export prices remain well supported but have softened in recent
months. The weakness has been concentrated in lean beef trimming prices,

which have eased since April as high US prices attracted additional imports,
particularly from South America, while tariff-free quotas temporarily boosted
product availability.
• In contrast, premium beef prices have remained firm, supported by tight global
supplies of high-quality beef, constrained US cattle numbers, and resilient
demand in higher-end retail and foodservice channels.
• We expect that lean trimming prices will reverse recent declines in coming

months as temporary increases in supply begin to fade and underlying
shortages, including the historically small US beef herd, re-emerge.
• In 2027, lean trimming prices should remain elevated but broadly flat as
strong US demand and a small US cattle herd are largely offset by rising global
production. From 2028 onwards, expanding global beef production gradually
eases market conditions, putting pressure on prices.
• Meanwhile, favourable supply/demand fundamentals to support premium beef

prices in 2027, with expanding global production to weigh on prices from 2028.
• Overall, beef export prices are likely to remain high, although both nominal and
real prices are expected to moderate over the medium term.

Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
TWI weighted price
Lamb export prices to remain firm, but off recent highs.
• NZ sheepmeat export prices remain near record highs, supported by
constrained global supply and resilient demand from premium markets in

Europe, the UK and North America. Export values rose strongly through 2025/26
despite only modest growth in export volumes, highlighting the strength of
underlying prices.
• Global supply remains tight due to declining sheep flocks and lower slaughter
numbers in New Zealand, the UK and parts of Europe. However, rising Australian
production and exports are expected to gradually lift global supply and increase
competition in key export markets, limiting further price gains.

• Meanwhile, stronger demand from the UK, EU and North America has improved
export returns and reduced New Zealand's reliance on China, where economic
weakness has made consumers more price sensitive and encouraged a shift
towards cheaper proteins and cuts.
• The outlook remains positive but more balanced. Tight supply should keep
prices above historical averages over the coming year, although rising Australian
exports, softer demand growth, affordability pressures, signs of buyer

resistance and (eventually) a stronger NZ dollar could see prices ease modestly
from current elevated levels into 2027.

Capacity is catching up with demand, limiting further price gains.
• NZ kiwifruit export prices have pushed to record highs, supported by strong
demand in North America, Europe, Japan and Korea, as well as continued

consumer preference for premium varieties such as SunGold.
• Export prices continue to edge higher even as a record crop and larger fruit
volumes are helping to reduce market tightness. Zespri notes increased
competition from summer fruit, pricing pressure in parts of Asia and a larger-
than-expected crop.
• Kiwifruit export prices are set to remain high but mostly flat over the next
year, as strong demand for premium varieties such as SunGold and RubyRed is

increasingly offset by rising production and price pressures in Asia.
• Apple export prices are also at record highs, supported by strong demand in
Asia, North America and India, alongside a continued shift towards higher-value
cultivars and premium varieties.
• These factors should keep export prices elevated over the next 12 months,
supporting grower revenues and profitability.
• However, further gains could be limited. Rising New Zealand production,

recovering supplies from key growing regions and larger global apple crops are
expected to increase supply availability, reducing market tightness and keeping
prices broadly stable.

Source: AgriHQ
Index IndexLog prices look moribund.
• China remains the dominant market for New Zealand logs, with prices highly
sensitive to construction activity, sawmill demand and daily log offtake. In that
regard, weak housing and furniture demand continue to weigh on prices.

• Chinese softwood log inventories are estimated at around 2.2 to 2.5m m³,
equivalent to roughly 40 to 45 days of inventory cover at current offtake rates.
This suggests inventory levels are broadly balanced and exerting little pressure
on log prices.
• At the same time, reduced New Zealand log supply, including lower harvesting
volumes and the wind-down of salvage logging, is providing some support to
prices. Freight costs also remain an important driver of at-wharf-gate returns,

with currently high shipping costs crimping returns to forest owners.
• NZ log export prices are expected to remain broadly stable to modestly firmer
over the next 12 months, supported by lower domestic supply, balanced Chinese
inventory levels and tentative signs of stabilisation in log demand.
• However, any recovery is likely to be gradual, with weakness in China's property
sector, uncertainty around shipping costs and softer global growth continuing to

constrain demand and cap upside potential.

## Document 360: RBNZ to hike next week as inflation risks mount

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBNZ to hike next week as inflation risks mount.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect a 25bp hike on 28 October by the Reserve Bank of
New Zealand. Since the dovish September meeting, rising oil
prices, higher front-end rates globally and weakness in the NZ
dollar all point to risks of inﬂation overshooting 4.0% in 4Q. Oﬀ-
meeting commentary has supported market bets on another
hike, and we expect a hawkish-leaning statement
Francesco Pesole
Auckland, New Zealand

### Page 2

In September, the Reserve Bank of New Zealand raised rates to
2.75% but pushed back against expectations for a large tightening
cycle. Its projections pointed to the next hike only in December or
1Q27 and a 3.25% peak in 2028-2029.
The picture has turned increasingly hawkish since then. We now
expect a hike on 28 October, in line with market pricing,
accompanied by a hawkish statement. We expect another hike in
either December or February, with a slight preference for February,

and see chances of another move later in 2027. Our terminal rate
estimate is 3.25-3.5%.
Markets input has been hawkish for the
RBNZ
Source: ING, Macrobond
A lot has changed since September
Third-quarter inﬂation slowed from 4.1% to 3.7%, below both the
RBNZ’s 3.9% estimate and our 3.8% forecast. Non-tradable CPI, a
measure of core inﬂation, came in at 3.3% versus the RBNZ’s 3.6%
estimate. In isolation, those numbers would have reinforced
September’s dovish message and argued for an October hold.

Other developments, however, have argued for earlier action:
1. Higher energy prices. The RBNZ’s September projections
assumed oil prices would gradually decline. Instead, they have
risen around 20% since the September meeting. That has
materially increased the chances of 4Q inﬂation exceeding

### Page 3

4.0%, compared with the RBNZ’s 3.9% estimate, and remaining
stickier afterwards.
2. Weaker Kiwi dollar. The trade-weighted value of NZD tumbled
4% in September, and is at the lowest since 2009 (chart below).
The RBNZ has been one of the culprits behind a weak NZD, and
while it doesn’t normally react to FX moves, the depreciation is
increasing imported inﬂation risk.
3. Resilient growth. Second-quarter GDP surprised to the upside
at 2.6% YoY, against the RBNZ’s 2.1% estimate. Activity surveys

suggest growth may remain around 2.0% in both 3Q and 4Q,
leaving the RBNZ more room to ﬁght inﬂation with higher rates.
4. Hawkish repricing elsewhere. US two-year swap rates have
risen around 35bp since the start of September, with NZD 2yr
swaps following 33bp higher. Australia’s increase was smaller
at 15bp, but so is the starting point for rates. The RBA raised
rates again to 4.6% in September and kept the door open for
more.
Kiwi dollar at the lowest since the GFC

Source: ING, Macrobond
Hawkish comments seemed to greenlight
October move
RBNZ Chief Economist Paul Conway’s comments on 15 October
helped cement market expectations for an October hike despite
softer 3Q inﬂation. He described this month’s meeting as “wide
open”, contrary to what the rate projections had suggested,

acknowledged market pricing and highlighted upside risks to the
3.9% 4Q inﬂation projection from higher oil prices and NZD
depreciation.
The focus on 4Q inﬂation seemed to us to be a signal for markets
to look past the softer 3Q data. Governor Anna Breman reinforced
that message today, highlighting concerns about a higher 4Q
ﬁgure and eﬀectively neutralising any dovish impact from the
release. Markets now price in 21bp for this meeting. The proximity

of New Zealand’s 7 November general election does not seem a
suﬃcient reason for the RBNZ to deliver a big dovish surprise by
holding rates.
Hawkish hike to help NZD
We see little reason for the RBNZ to hike and pair the move with
dovish signals. Renewed focus on the currency means its
communication may be more market-aware than usual, while
resilient growth allows it to err on the hawkish side.
There are no new projections at this meeting, giving the RBNZ more

ﬂexibility to adjust its policy message through the statement
without sounding committal on the rate path. We think the
message will focus on a) keeping the door open to a December
hike; and b) signalling that the hiking cycle can be longer and faster
than previously estimated. We expect this to validate market
pricing for around 75bp of tightening by end-1Q27.
We saw good upside potential for NZD in our latest monthly FX
update, as the currency looked cheap by short-term valuation

metrics and there was room for further hawkish repricing in NZD
front-end rates. Our 0.570 one-month target has already been
reached, and we admit further NZD/USD gains depend more on
global/US developments than domestic factors. Still, the pair is
roughly 1% undervalued in the short term, and some emerging
USD idiosyncratic weakness around AI concerns, combined with a
hawkish RBNZ hike, can help build a stronger ﬂoor and leave
potential for further exploration.

## Document 403: WEEKLY WEBCAST_ Question For The Fed_ Where Is Waldo_

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
Date:    3 hours ago
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question
For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
By Ed Yardeni • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

The risks to the Fed’s dual mandate are constantly changing. Its attempt
to hit its inflation target is complicated by price pressures beyond the
control of interest rates, and its attempt to hit on a federal funds rate that
neither over-stimulates nor over-restricts economic growth is complicated
by the elusive nature of this neutral rate. Today, Elias and Ed examine the
forces with bearing on the Fed’s rate decisions since 2022. They also
explain why they believe that the neutral federal funds rate has risen over
recent years and the implications for monetary policy looking ahead.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable,
